> DEVELOPER

Implement the following plan:

# Fix: Empty repo "reference not found" error (#242)

## Context

In a freshly `git init`'d repo with no commits, `repo.Head()` returns `plumbing.ErrReferenceNotFound`. Both strategies fail during session initialization, producing a cryptic error: `"failed to save changes: failed to initialize session: failed to get HEAD: reference not found"`. The fix: detect the empty-repo case early, provide clear messaging, and degrade gracefully.

## Changes

### 1. Add `ErrEmptyRepository` sentinel error

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/strategy.go` (after line 27)

Add alongside existing sentinel errors:
```go
var ErrEmptyRepository = errors.New("repository has no commits yet")
```

### 2. Add `IsEmptyRepository` helper

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go`

```go
func IsEmptyRepository(repo *git.Repository) bool {
    _, err := repo.Head()
    return errors.Is(err, plumbing.ErrReferenceNotFound)
}
```

### 3. Fix manual-commit `initializeSession`

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go:195-198`

Replace generic error with empty-repo detection:
```go
head, err := repo.Head()
if err != nil {
    if errors.Is(err, plumbing.ErrReferenceNotFound) {
        return nil, fmt.Errorf("%w: create an initial commit to enable session checkpoints", ErrEmptyRepository)
    }
    return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
}
```

### 4. Fix auto-commit `InitializeSession`

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go:908-911`

Same pattern:
```go
head, err := repo.Head()
if err != nil {
    if errors.Is(err, plumbing.ErrReferenceNotFound) {
        return fmt.Errorf("%w: create an initial commit to enable session checkpoints", ErrEmptyRepository)
    }
    return fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
}
```

### 5. Improve hook handler messaging

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:82-84`

Detect `ErrEmptyRepository` and print a friendlier note instead of the generic warning:
```go
if err := initializer.InitializeSession(...); err != nil {
    if errors.Is(err, strategy.ErrEmptyRepository) {
        fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "Note: Session checkpoints disabled (no commits yet). Create your first commit to enable them.")
    } else {
        fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to initialize session state: %v\n", err)
    }
}
```

Add `"errors"` to imports (strategy is already imported).

### 6. Add warning in `entire enable`

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/setup.go` — in `RunE` (after git repo check at line 74)

Add a lightweight check right after the existing `paths.RepoRoot()` validation:
```go
// Warn if repo has no commits yet
if repo, err := strategy.OpenRepository(); err == nil && strategy.IsEmptyRepository(repo) {
    fmt.Fprintln(cmd.OutOrStdout(), "Note: This repository has no commits yet. Entire will be configured, but")
    fmt.Fprintln(cmd.OutOrStdout(), "session checkpoints won't work until you create your first commit.")
    fmt.Fprintln(cmd.OutOrStdout())
}
```

Add `"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy"` to imports.

### 7. Unit tests

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_test.go` — new test function

Test that `initializeSession` returns `ErrEmptyRepository` on an empty repo. Uses `t.TempDir()`, `git.PlainInit()`, and `t.Chdir()` (no `t.Parallel()` since `t.Chdir` is process-global).

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit_test.go` — new test function

Same pattern for auto-commit's `InitializeSession`.

**File:** `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common_test.go` — new test function

Test `IsEmptyRepository` returns true for empty repo, false for repo with commits.

## Files touched

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `strategy/strategy.go` | Add `ErrEmptyRepository` sentinel |
| `strategy/common.go` | Add `IsEmptyRepository()` helper |
| `strategy/manual_commit_session.go` | Detect empty repo in `initializeSession` |
| `strategy/auto_commit.go` | Detect empty repo in `InitializeSession` |
| `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` | Friendly message for empty repo |
| `setup.go` | Warning during `entire enable` |
| `strategy/manual_commit_test.go` | Unit test |
| `strategy/auto_commit_test.go` | Unit test |
| `strategy/common_test.go` | Unit test |

## What this does NOT change

- No attempt to fully support empty repos (too many downstream HEAD dependencies)
- No changes to `SaveChanges` or `SaveTaskCheckpoint` — the improved error from `initializeSession` flows through naturally
- No changes to other `repo.Head()` callers — they're only reachable with an initialized session, which can't happen in an empty repo

## Verification

```bash
mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci
```

Manual test:
1. `mkdir /tmp/test-empty && cd /tmp/test-empty && git init`
2. `entire enable` — should succeed with warning about no commits
3. Start a Claude Code session — should see "Note: Session checkpoints disabled" instead of the cryptic error
4. `git commit --allow-empty -m "Initial commit"` — subsequent sessions should work normally

    44→// Returns empty string if not a task metadata directory.
    45→func ExtractToolUseIDFromTaskMetadataDir(metadataDir string) string {
    46→	parts := strings.Split(metadataDir, "/")
    47→	if len(parts) >= 2 && parts[len(parts)-2] == "tasks" {

   285→// TaskMetadataDir returns the path to a task's metadata directory

   290→
   291→// ReadTaskCheckpoint reads the checkpoint.json file from a task metadata directory.
   292→// This is used during rewind to get the checkpoint UUID for transcript truncation.
   293→func ReadTaskCheckpoint(taskMetadataDir string) (*TaskCheckpoint, error) {
   294→	checkpointFile := taskMetadataDir + "/checkpoint.json"
   295→
   296→	data, err := os.ReadFile(checkpointFile) //nolint:gosec // Reading from controlled git metadata path
   297→	if err != nil {
   298→		return nil, err //nolint:wrapcheck // already present in codebase
   299→	}
   300→
   301→	var checkpoint TaskCheckpoint
   302→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &checkpoint); err != nil {
   303→		return nil, err //nolint:wrapcheck // already present in codebase
   304→	}
   305→
   306→	return &checkpoint, nil
   307→}

   309→// Strategy defines the interface for git operation strategies.
   310→// Different implementations can use commits, branches, stashes, etc.
   311→//
   312→// Note: State capture (tracking untracked files before a session) is handled
   313→// by the CLI layer, not the strategy. The strategy receives pre-computed
   314→// file lists in SaveContext.
   315→type Strategy interface {
   316→	// Name returns the strategy identifier (e.g., "commit", "branch", "stash")
   317→	Name() string
   318→
   319→	// Description returns a human-readable description for the setup wizard
   320→	Description() string
   321→
   322→	// ValidateRepository checks if the repository is in a valid state
   323→	// for this strategy to operate. Returns an error if validation fails.
   324→	ValidateRepository() error
   325→
   326→	// SaveChanges is called on Stop to save all session changes
   327→	// using this strategy's approach (commit, branch, stash, etc.)
   328→	SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext) error
   329→
   330→	// SaveTaskCheckpoint is called by PostToolUse[Task] hook when a subagent completes.
   331→	// Creates a checkpoint commit with task metadata for later rewind.
   332→	// Different strategies may handle this differently:
   333→	// - Commit strategy: commits to active branch
   334→	// - Manual-commit strategy: commits to shadow branch
   335→	// - Auto-commit strategy: commits logs to shadow only (code deferred to Stop)
   336→	SaveTaskCheckpoint(ctx TaskCheckpointContext) error
   337→
   338→	// GetRewindPoints returns available points to rewind to.
   339→	// The limit parameter controls the maximum number of points to return.
   340→	GetRewindPoints(limit int) ([]RewindPoint, error)
   341→
   342→	// Rewind restores the repository to the given rewind point.
   343→	// The metadataDir in the point is used to restore the session transcript.
   344→	Rewind(point RewindPoint) error
   345→
   346→	// CanRewind checks if rewinding is currently possible.
   347→	// Returns (canRewind, reason if not, error)
   348→	CanRewind() (bool, string, error)
   349→
   350→	// PreviewRewind returns what will happen if rewinding to the given point.
   351→	// This allows showing warnings about files that will be deleted before the rewind.
   352→	// Returns nil if preview is not supported (e.g., auto-commit strategy).
   353→	PreviewRewind(point RewindPoint) (*RewindPreview, error)
   354→
   355→	// GetTaskCheckpoint returns the task checkpoint for a given rewind point.
   356→	// For strategies that store checkpoints in git (auto-commit), this reads from the branch.
   357→	// For strategies that store checkpoints on disk (commit, manual-commit), this reads from the filesystem.
   358→	// Returns nil, nil if not a task checkpoint or checkpoint not found.
   359→	GetTaskCheckpoint(point RewindPoint) (*TaskCheckpoint, error)
   360→
   361→	// GetTaskCheckpointTranscript returns the session transcript for a task checkpoint.
   362→	// For strategies that store transcripts in git (auto-commit), this reads from the branch.
   363→	// For strategies that store transcripts on disk (commit, manual-commit), this reads from the filesystem.
   364→	GetTaskCheckpointTranscript(point RewindPoint) ([]byte, error)
   365→
   366→	// GetSessionInfo returns session information for linking commits.
   367→	// This is used by the context command to generate trailers.
   368→	// Returns ErrNoSession if no session info is available.
   369→	GetSessionInfo() (*SessionInfo, error)
   370→
   371→	// EnsureSetup ensures the strategy's required setup is in place,
   372→	// installing any missing pieces (git hooks, gitignore entries, etc.).
   373→	// Returns nil if setup is complete or was successfully installed.
   374→	EnsureSetup() error
   375→
   376→	// NOTE: ListSessions and GetSession are standalone functions in session.go.
   377→	// They read from entire/checkpoints/v1 and merge with SessionSource if implemented.
   378→
   379→	// GetMetadataRef returns a reference to the metadata commit for the given checkpoint.
   380→	// Format: "<branch>@<commit-sha>" (e.g., "entire/checkpoints/v1@abc123").
   381→	// Returns empty string if not applicable (e.g., commit strategy with filesystem metadata).
   382→	GetMetadataRef(checkpoint Checkpoint) string
   383→
   384→	// GetSessionMetadataRef returns a reference to the most recent metadata commit for a session.
   385→	// Format: "<branch>@<commit-sha>" (e.g., "entire/checkpoints/v1@abc123").
   386→	// Returns empty string if not applicable or session not found.
   387→	GetSessionMetadataRef(sessionID string) string
   388→
   389→	// GetSessionContext returns the context.md content for a session.
   390→	// Returns empty string if not available.
   391→	GetSessionContext(sessionID string) string
   392→
   393→	// GetCheckpointLog returns the session transcript for a specific checkpoint.
   394→	// For strategies that store transcripts in git branches (auto-commit, manual-commit),
   395→	// this reads from the checkpoint's commit tree.
   396→	// For strategies that store on disk (commit), reads from the filesystem.
   397→	// Returns ErrNoMetadata if transcript is not available.
   398→	GetCheckpointLog(checkpoint Checkpoint) ([]byte, error)
   399→}
   400→
   401→// SessionInitializer is an optional interface for strategies that need to
   402→// initialize session state when a user prompt is submitted.
   403→// Strategies like manual-commit use this to create session state files that
   404→// the git prepare-commit-msg hook can detect.
   405→type SessionInitializer interface {
   406→	// InitializeSession creates session state for a new session.
   407→	// Called during UserPromptSubmit hook before any checkpoints are created.
   408→	// agentType is the human-readable name of the agent (e.g., "Claude Code").
   409→	// transcriptPath is the path to the live transcript file (for mid-session commit detection).
   410→	// userPrompt is the user's prompt text (stored truncated as FirstPrompt for display).
   411→	InitializeSession(sessionID string, agentType agent.AgentType, transcriptPath string, userPrompt string) error
   412→}
   413→
   414→// PrepareCommitMsgHandler is an optional interface for strategies that need to
   415→// handle the git prepare-commit-msg hook.
   416→type PrepareCommitMsgHandler interface {
   417→	// PrepareCommitMsg is called by the git prepare-commit-msg hook.
   418→	// It can modify the commit message file to add trailers, etc.
   419→	// The source parameter indicates how the commit was initiated:
   420→	//   - "" or "template": normal editor flow
   421→	//   - "message": using -m or -F flag
   422→	//   - "merge": merge commit
   423→	//   - "squash": squash merge
   424→	//   - "commit": amend with -c/-C
   425→	// Should return nil on errors to not block commits (log warnings to stderr).
   426→	PrepareCommitMsg(commitMsgFile string, source string) error
   427→}
   428→
   429→// PostCommitHandler is an optional interface for strategies that need to
   430→// handle the git post-commit hook.
   431→type PostCommitHandler interface {
   432→	// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   433→	// Used to perform actions like condensing session data after commits.
   434→	// Should return nil on errors to not block subsequent operations (log warnings to stderr).
   435→	PostCommit() error
   436→}
   437→
   438→// CommitMsgHandler is an optional interface for strategies that need to
   439→// handle the git commit-msg hook.
   440→type CommitMsgHandler interface {
   441→	// CommitMsg is called by the git commit-msg hook after the user edits the message.
   442→	// Used to validate or modify the final commit message before the commit is created.
   443→	// If this returns an error, the commit is aborted.
   444→	CommitMsg(commitMsgFile string) error
   445→}
   446→
   447→// PrePushHandler is an optional interface for strategies that need to
   448→// handle the git pre-push hook.
   449→type PrePushHandler interface {
   450→	// PrePush is called by the git pre-push hook before pushing to a remote.
   451→	// Used to push session branches (e.g., entire/checkpoints/v1) alongside user pushes.
   452→	// The remote parameter is the name of the remote being pushed to.
   453→	// Should return nil on errors to not block pushes (log warnings to stderr).
   454→	PrePush(remote string) error
   455→}
   456→
   457→// TurnEndHandler is an optional interface for strategies that need to
   458→// handle deferred actions when an agent turn ends.
   459→// For example, manual-commit strategy uses this to condense session data
   460→// that was deferred during ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE transitions.
   461→type TurnEndHandler interface {
   462→	// HandleTurnEnd dispatches strategy-specific actions emitted by the
   463→	// ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE (or other) turn-end transition.
   464→	// The state has already been updated by ApplyCommonActions; the caller
   465→	// saves it after this method returns.
   466→	HandleTurnEnd(state *session.State, actions []session.Action) error
   467→}
   468→
   469→// RestoredSession describes a single session that was restored by RestoreLogsOnly.
   470→// Each session may come from a different agent, so callers use this to print
   471→// per-session resume commands without re-reading the metadata tree.
   472→type RestoredSession struct {
   473→	SessionID string
   474→	Agent     agent.AgentType
   475→	Prompt    string
   476→}
   477→
   478→// LogsOnlyRestorer is an optional interface for strategies that support
   479→// restoring session logs without file state restoration.
   480→// This is used for "logs-only" rewind points where only the session transcript
   481→// can be restored (file state requires git checkout).
   482→type LogsOnlyRestorer interface {
   483→	// RestoreLogsOnly restores session logs from a logs-only rewind point.
   484→	// Does not modify the working directory - only restores the transcript
   485→	// to the agent's session directory (determined per-session from checkpoint metadata).
   486→	// If force is false, prompts for confirmation when local logs have newer timestamps.
   487→	// Returns info about each restored session so callers can print correct resume commands.
   488→	RestoreLogsOnly(point RewindPoint, force bool) ([]RestoredSession, error)
   489→}
   490→
   491→// SessionResetter is an optional interface for strategies that support
   492→// resetting session state and shadow branches.
   493→// This is used by the "reset" command to clean up shadow branches
   494→// and session state when a user wants to start fresh.
   495→type SessionResetter interface {
   496→	// Reset deletes the shadow branch and session state for the current HEAD.
   497→	// Returns nil if there's nothing to reset (no shadow branch).
   498→	Reset() error
   499→
   500→	// ResetSession clears the state for a single session and cleans up
   501→	// the shadow branch if no other sessions reference it.
   502→	// File changes remain in the working directory.
   503→	ResetSession(sessionID string) error
   504→}
   505→
   506→// SessionCondenser is an optional interface for strategies that support
   507→// force-condensing a session. This is used by "entire doctor" to
   508→// salvage stuck sessions by condensing their data to permanent storage.
   509→type SessionCondenser interface {
   510→	// CondenseSessionByID force-condenses a session and cleans up.
   511→	// Generates a new checkpoint ID, condenses to entire/checkpoints/v1,
   512→	// updates the session state, and removes the shadow branch
   513→	// if no other active sessions need it.
   514→	CondenseSessionByID(sessionID string) error
   515→}
   516→
   517→// ConcurrentSessionChecker is an optional interface for strategies that support
   518→// counting concurrent sessions with uncommitted changes.
   519→// This is used by the SessionStart hook to show an informational message about
   520→// how many other active conversations will be included in the next commit.
   521→type ConcurrentSessionChecker interface {
   522→	// CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints returns the number of other active sessions
   523→	// with uncommitted checkpoints on the same base commit.
   524→	// Returns 0, nil if no such sessions exist.
   525→	CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints(currentSessionID string) (int, error)
   526→}
   527→
   528→// SessionSource is an optional interface for strategies that provide additional
   529→// sessions beyond those stored on the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
   530→// For example, manual-commit strategy provides active sessions from .git/entire-sessions/
   531→// that haven't yet been condensed to entire/checkpoints/v1.
   532→//
   533→// ListSessions() automatically discovers all registered strategies, checks if they
   534→// implement SessionSource, and merges their additional sessions by ID.
   535→type SessionSource interface {
   536→	// GetAdditionalSessions returns sessions not yet on entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
   537→	GetAdditionalSessions() ([]*Session, error)
   538→}
   539→
   540→// OrphanedItemsLister is an optional interface for strategies that can identify
   541→// orphaned items (shadow branches, session states, checkpoints) that should be
   542→// cleaned up. This is used by the "entire session cleanup" command.
   543→//
   544→// ListAllCleanupItems() automatically discovers all registered strategies, checks
   545→// if they implement OrphanedItemsLister, and combines their orphaned items.
   546→type OrphanedItemsLister interface {
   547→	// ListOrphanedItems returns items created by this strategy that are now orphaned.
   548→	// Each strategy defines what "orphaned" means for its own data structures.
   549→	ListOrphanedItems() ([]CleanupItem, error)
   550→}

    35→// errStop is a sentinel error used to break out of git log iteration.
    36→// Shared across strategies that iterate through git commits.
    37→// NOTE: A similar sentinel exists in checkpoint/temporary.go - this is intentional.
    38→// Each package needs its own package-scoped sentinel for git log iteration patterns.
    39→var errStop = errors.New("stop iteration")
    40→
    41→// IsAncestorOf checks if commit is an ancestor of (or equal to) target.
    42→// Returns true if target can reach commit by following parent links.
    43→// Limits search to 1000 commits to avoid excessive traversal.
    44→func IsAncestorOf(repo *git.Repository, commit, target plumbing.Hash) bool {
    45→	if commit == target {
    46→		return true
    47→	}
    48→
    49→	iter, err := repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: target})
    50→	if err != nil {
    51→		return false
    52→	}
    53→	defer iter.Close()
    54→
    55→	found := false
    56→	count := 0
    57→	_ = iter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error { //nolint:errcheck // Best-effort search, errors are non-fatal
    58→		count++
    59→		if count > 1000 {
    60→			return errStop
    61→		}
    62→		if c.Hash == commit {
    63→			found = true
    64→			return errStop
    65→		}
    66→		return nil
    67→	})
    68→
    69→	return found
    70→}
    71→
    72→// ListCheckpoints returns all checkpoints from the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
    73→// Scans sharded paths: <id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/ directories containing metadata.json.
    74→// Used by both manual-commit and auto-commit strategies.
    75→func ListCheckpoints() ([]CheckpointInfo, error) {
    76→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
    77→	if err != nil {
    78→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
    79→	}
    80→
    81→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
    82→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
    83→	if err != nil {
    84→		//nolint:nilerr // No sessions branch yet is expected, return empty list
    85→		return []CheckpointInfo{}, nil
    86→	}
    87→
    88→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
    89→	if err != nil {
    90→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit object: %w", err)
    91→	}
    92→
    93→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
    94→	if err != nil {
    95→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit tree: %w", err)
    96→	}
    97→
    98→	var checkpoints []CheckpointInfo
    99→
   100→	// Scan sharded structure: <2-char-prefix>/<remaining-id>/metadata.json
   101→	// The tree has 2-character directories (hex buckets)
   102→	for _, bucketEntry := range tree.Entries {
   103→		if bucketEntry.Mode != filemode.Dir {
   104→			continue
   105→		}
   106→		// Bucket should be 2 hex chars
   107→		if len(bucketEntry.Name) != 2 {
   108→			continue
   109→		}
   110→
   111→		bucketTree, treeErr := repo.TreeObject(bucketEntry.Hash)
   112→		if treeErr != nil {
   113→			continue
   114→		}
   115→
   116→		// Each entry in the bucket is the remaining part of the checkpoint ID
   117→		for _, checkpointEntry := range bucketTree.Entries {
   118→			if checkpointEntry.Mode != filemode.Dir {
   119→				continue
   120→			}
   121→
   122→			checkpointTree, cpTreeErr := repo.TreeObject(checkpointEntry.Hash)
   123→			if cpTreeErr != nil {
   124→				continue
   125→			}
   126→
   127→			// Reconstruct checkpoint ID: <bucket><remaining>
   128→			checkpointIDStr := bucketEntry.Name + checkpointEntry.Name
   129→			checkpointID, cpErr := id.NewCheckpointID(checkpointIDStr)
   130→			if cpErr != nil {
   131→				// Skip invalid checkpoint IDs
   132→				continue
   133→			}
   134→
   135→			info := CheckpointInfo{
   136→				CheckpointID: checkpointID,
   137→			}
   138→
   139→			// Get details from metadata file (CheckpointSummary format)
   140→			if metadataFile, fileErr := checkpointTree.File(paths.MetadataFileName); fileErr == nil {
   141→				if content, contentErr := metadataFile.Contents(); contentErr == nil {
   142→					var summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary
   143→					if json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary) == nil && len(summary.Sessions) > 0 {
   144→						info.CheckpointsCount = summary.CheckpointsCount
   145→						info.FilesTouched = summary.FilesTouched
   146→						info.SessionCount = len(summary.Sessions)
   147→
   148→						// Read session-level metadata for Agent, SessionID, CreatedAt, SessionIDs
   149→						for i, sessionPaths := range summary.Sessions {
   150→							if sessionPaths.Metadata != "" {
   151→								// SessionFilePaths now contains absolute paths with leading "/"
   152→								// Strip the leading "/" for tree.File() which expects paths without leading slash
   153→								sessionMetadataPath := strings.TrimPrefix(sessionPaths.Metadata, "/")
   154→								if sessionFile, sErr := tree.File(sessionMetadataPath); sErr == nil {
   155→									if sessionContent, scErr := sessionFile.Contents(); scErr == nil {
   156→										var sessionMetadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
   157→										if json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionContent), &sessionMetadata) == nil {
   158→											info.SessionIDs = append(info.SessionIDs, sessionMetadata.SessionID)
   159→											// Use first session's metadata for Agent, SessionID, CreatedAt
   160→											if i == 0 {
   161→												info.Agent = sessionMetadata.Agent
   162→												info.SessionID = sessionMetadata.SessionID
   163→												info.CreatedAt = sessionMetadata.CreatedAt
   164→												info.IsTask = sessionMetadata.IsTask
   165→												info.ToolUseID = sessionMetadata.ToolUseID
   166→											}
   167→										}
   168→									}
   169→								}
   170→							}
   171→						}
   172→					}
   173→				}
   174→			}
   175→
   176→			checkpoints = append(checkpoints, info)
   177→		}
   178→	}
   179→
   180→	// Sort by time (most recent first)
   181→	sort.Slice(checkpoints, func(i, j int) bool {
   182→		return checkpoints[i].CreatedAt.After(checkpoints[j].CreatedAt)
   183→	})
   184→
   185→	return checkpoints, nil
   186→}

   198→// isProtectedPath returns true if relPath is inside a directory that should
   199→// never be modified or deleted during rewind or other destructive operations.
   200→// Protected directories include git internals, entire metadata, and all
   201→// registered agent config directories.
   202→func isProtectedPath(relPath string) bool {
   203→	for _, dir := range protectedDirs() {
   204→		if relPath == dir || strings.HasPrefix(relPath, dir+string(filepath.Separator)) {
   205→			return true
   206→		}
   207→	}
   208→	return false
   209→}
   210→
   211→// protectedDirs returns the list of directories to protect. This combines
   212→// static infrastructure dirs with agent-reported dirs from the registry.
   213→// The result is cached via sync.Once since it's called per-file during filepath.Walk.
   214→//
   215→// NOTE: The cache is never invalidated. In production this is fine (the agent registry
   216→// is populated at init time and never changes). However, tests that mutate the agent
   217→// registry after the first call to protectedDirs/isProtectedPath will see stale results.
   218→// If you need to test isProtectedPath with a custom registry, either:
   219→//   - run those tests in a separate process, or
   220→//   - call resetProtectedDirsForTest() to clear the cache.
   221→func protectedDirs() []string {
   222→	protectedDirsOnce.Do(func() {
   223→		protectedDirsCache = append([]string{gitDir, entireDir}, agent.AllProtectedDirs()...)
   224→	})
   225→	return protectedDirsCache
   226→}
   227→
   228→var (
   229→	protectedDirsOnce  sync.Once
   230→	protectedDirsCache []string
   231→)
   232→
   233→// homeRelativePath strips the $HOME/ prefix from an absolute path,
   234→// returning a home-relative path suitable for persisting in metadata.
   235→// Returns "" if the path is empty or not under $HOME.
   236→func homeRelativePath(absPath string) string {
   237→	if absPath == "" {
   238→		return ""
   239→	}
   240→	home, err := os.UserHomeDir()
   241→	if err != nil || home == "" {
   242→		return ""
   243→	}
   244→	prefix := home + string(filepath.Separator)
   245→	if !strings.HasPrefix(absPath, prefix) {
   246→		return ""
   247→	}
   248→	return absPath[len(prefix):]
   249→}
   250→
   251→// isSpecificAgentType returns true if the agent type is a known, specific value
   252→// (not empty and not the generic "Agent" fallback).
   253→func isSpecificAgentType(t agent.AgentType) bool {
   254→	return t != "" && t != DefaultAgentType
   255→}
   256→
   257→// resolveAgentType picks the best agent type from the context and existing state.
   258→// Priority: existing state (if specific) > context value > default fallback.
   259→func resolveAgentType(ctxAgentType agent.AgentType, state *SessionState) agent.AgentType {
   260→	if state != nil && isSpecificAgentType(state.AgentType) {
   261→		return state.AgentType
   262→	}
   263→	if ctxAgentType != "" {
   264→		return ctxAgentType
   265→	}
   266→	return DefaultAgentType
   267→}

   269→// ensureMetadataBranch creates the orphan entire/checkpoints/v1 branch if it doesn't exist.
   270→// This branch has no parent and starts with an empty tree.
   271→func EnsureMetadataBranch(repo *git.Repository) error {
   272→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
   273→
   274→	// Check if branch already exists
   275→	_, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   276→	if err == nil {
   277→		// Branch already exists
   278→		return nil
   279→	}
   280→
   281→	// Create empty tree (no files)
   282→	emptyTree := &object.Tree{Entries: []object.TreeEntry{}}
   283→	obj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   284→	if err := emptyTree.Encode(obj); err != nil {
   285→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to encode empty tree: %w", err)
   286→	}
   287→	emptyTreeHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)
   288→	if err != nil {
   289→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to store empty tree: %w", err)
   290→	}
   291→
   292→	// Create orphan commit (no parent)
   293→	now := time.Now()
   294→	sig := object.Signature{
   295→		Name:  "Entire",
   296→		Email: "entire@local",
   297→		When:  now,
   298→	}
   299→
   300→	commit := &object.Commit{
   301→		TreeHash:  emptyTreeHash,
   302→		Author:    sig,
   303→		Committer: sig,
   304→		Message:   "Initialize metadata branch\n\nThis branch stores session metadata for the auto-commit strategy.\n",
   305→	}
   306→	// Note: No ParentHashes - this is an orphan commit
   307→
   308→	commitObj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   309→	if err := commit.Encode(commitObj); err != nil {
   310→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to encode orphan commit: %w", err)
   311→	}
   312→	commitHash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(commitObj)
   313→	if err != nil {
   314→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to store orphan commit: %w", err)
   315→	}
   316→
   317→	// Create branch reference
   318→	ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(refName, commitHash)
   319→	if err := repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   320→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create metadata branch: %w", err)
   321→	}
   322→
   323→	fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "✓ Created orphan branch '%s' for session metadata\n", paths.MetadataBranchName)
   324→	return nil
   325→}
   326→
   327→// readCheckpointMetadata reads metadata.json from a checkpoint path on entire/checkpoints/v1.
   328→// With the new format, root metadata.json is a CheckpointSummary with Agents array.
   329→// This function reads the summary and extracts relevant fields into CheckpointInfo,
   330→// also reading session-level metadata for IsTask/ToolUseID fields.
   331→func ReadCheckpointMetadata(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string) (*CheckpointInfo, error) {
   332→	metadataPath := checkpointPath + "/metadata.json"
   333→	file, err := tree.File(metadataPath)
   334→	if err != nil {
   335→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to find metadata at %s: %w", metadataPath, err)
   336→	}
   337→
   338→	content, err := file.Contents()
   339→	if err != nil {
   340→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read metadata: %w", err)
   341→	}
   342→
   343→	// Try to parse as CheckpointSummary first (new format)
   344→	var summary checkpoint.CheckpointSummary
   345→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &summary); err == nil {
   346→		// If we have sessions array, this is the new format
   347→		if len(summary.Sessions) > 0 {
   348→			info := &CheckpointInfo{
   349→				CheckpointID:     summary.CheckpointID,
   350→				CheckpointsCount: summary.CheckpointsCount,
   351→				FilesTouched:     summary.FilesTouched,
   352→				SessionCount:     len(summary.Sessions),
   353→			}
   354→
   355→			// Read all sessions' metadata to populate SessionIDs and get other fields from first session
   356→			var sessionIDs []string
   357→			for i, sessionPaths := range summary.Sessions {
   358→				if sessionPaths.Metadata != "" {
   359→					// SessionFilePaths now contains absolute paths with leading "/"
   360→					// Strip the leading "/" for tree.File() which expects paths without leading slash
   361→					sessionMetadataPath := strings.TrimPrefix(sessionPaths.Metadata, "/")
   362→					if sessionFile, err := tree.File(sessionMetadataPath); err == nil {
   363→						if sessionContent, err := sessionFile.Contents(); err == nil {
   364→							var sessionMetadata checkpoint.CommittedMetadata
   365→							if json.Unmarshal([]byte(sessionContent), &sessionMetadata) == nil {
   366→								sessionIDs = append(sessionIDs, sessionMetadata.SessionID)
   367→								// Use first session for Agent, SessionID, CreatedAt, IsTask, ToolUseID
   368→								if i == 0 {
   369→									info.Agent = sessionMetadata.Agent
   370→									info.SessionID = sessionMetadata.SessionID
   371→									info.CreatedAt = sessionMetadata.CreatedAt
   372→									info.IsTask = sessionMetadata.IsTask
   373→									info.ToolUseID = sessionMetadata.ToolUseID
   374→								}
   375→							}
   376→						}
   377→					}
   378→				}
   379→			}
   380→			info.SessionIDs = sessionIDs
   381→
   382→			return info, nil
   383→		}
   384→	}
   385→
   386→	// Fall back to parsing as CheckpointInfo (old format or direct info)
   387→	var metadata CheckpointInfo
   388→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &metadata); err != nil {
   389→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse metadata: %w", err)
   390→	}
   391→
   392→	return &metadata, nil
   393→}

   395→// GetMetadataBranchTree returns the tree object for the entire/checkpoints/v1 branch.
   396→func GetMetadataBranchTree(repo *git.Repository) (*object.Tree, error) {
   397→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(paths.MetadataBranchName)
   398→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   399→	if err != nil {
   400→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get metadata branch reference: %w", err)
   401→	}
   402→
   403→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   404→	if err != nil {
   405→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get metadata branch commit: %w", err)
   406→	}

   408→	tree, err := commit.Tree()

   410→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get metadata branch tree: %w", err)

   412→	return tree, nil
   413→}
   414→
   415→// ExtractFirstPrompt extracts and truncates the first meaningful prompt from prompt content.
   416→// Prompts are separated by "\n\n---\n\n". Skips empty prompts and separator-only content.
   417→// Returns empty string if no valid prompt is found.
   418→func ExtractFirstPrompt(content string) string {
   419→	if content == "" {
   420→		return ""
   421→	}
   422→
   423→	// Prompts are separated by "\n\n---\n\n"
   424→	// Find the first non-empty prompt
   425→	prompts := strings.Split(content, "\n\n---\n\n")
   426→	var firstPrompt string
   427→	for _, p := range prompts {
   428→		cleaned := strings.TrimSpace(p)
   429→		// Skip empty prompts or prompts that are just dashes/separators
   430→		if cleaned == "" || isOnlySeparators(cleaned) {
   431→			continue
   432→		}
   433→		firstPrompt = cleaned
   434→		break
   435→	}
   436→
   437→	if firstPrompt == "" {
   438→		return ""
   439→	}
   440→
   441→	return TruncateDescription(firstPrompt, MaxDescriptionLength)
   442→}
   443→
   444→// ReadSessionPromptFromTree reads the first meaningful prompt from a checkpoint's prompt.txt file in a git tree.
   445→// Returns an empty string if the prompt cannot be read.
   446→func ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string) string {
   447→	promptPath := checkpointPath + "/" + paths.PromptFileName
   448→	file, err := tree.File(promptPath)
   449→	if err != nil {
   450→		return ""
   451→	}
   452→
   453→	content, err := file.Contents()
   454→	if err != nil {
   455→		return ""
   456→	}
   457→
   458→	return ExtractFirstPrompt(content)
   459→}
   460→
   461→// isOnlySeparators checks if a string contains only dashes, spaces, and newlines.
   462→func isOnlySeparators(s string) bool {
   463→	for _, r := range s {
   464→		if r != '-' && r != ' ' && r != '\n' && r != '\r' && r != '\t' {
   465→			return false
   466→		}
   467→	}
   468→	return true
   469→}
   470→
   471→// ReadAllSessionPromptsFromTree reads the first prompt for all sessions in a multi-session checkpoint.
   472→// Returns a slice of prompts parallel to sessionIDs (oldest to newest).
   473→// For single-session checkpoints, returns a slice with just the root prompt.
   474→func ReadAllSessionPromptsFromTree(tree *object.Tree, checkpointPath string, sessionCount int, sessionIDs []string) []string {
   475→	if sessionCount <= 1 || len(sessionIDs) <= 1 {
   476→		// Single session - just return the root prompt
   477→		prompt := ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, checkpointPath)
   478→		if prompt != "" {
   479→			return []string{prompt}
   480→		}
   481→		return nil
   482→	}
   483→
   484→	// Multi-session: read prompts from archived folders (0/, 1/, etc.) and root
   485→	prompts := make([]string, len(sessionIDs))
   486→
   487→	// Read archived session prompts (folders 0, 1, ... N-2)
   488→	for i := range sessionCount - 1 {
   489→		archivedPath := fmt.Sprintf("%s/%d", checkpointPath, i)
   490→		prompts[i] = ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, archivedPath)
   491→	}
   492→
   493→	// Read the most recent session prompt (at root level)
   494→	prompts[len(prompts)-1] = ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, checkpointPath)
   495→
   496→	return prompts
   497→}
   498→
   499→// ReadSessionPromptFromShadow reads the first prompt for a session from the shadow branch.
   500→// Returns an empty string if the prompt cannot be read.
   501→func ReadSessionPromptFromShadow(repo *git.Repository, baseCommit, worktreeID, sessionID string) string {
   502→	// Get shadow branch for this base commit using worktree-specific naming
   503→	shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   504→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName), true)
   505→	if err != nil {
   506→		return ""
   507→	}
   508→
   509→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   510→	if err != nil {
   511→		return ""
   512→	}
   513→
   514→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   515→	if err != nil {
   516→		return ""
   517→	}
   518→
   519→	// Build the path to prompt.txt: .entire/metadata/<session-id>/prompt.txt
   520→	checkpointPath := paths.EntireMetadataDir + "/" + sessionID
   521→	return ReadSessionPromptFromTree(tree, checkpointPath)
   522→}
   523→
   524→// GetRemoteMetadataBranchTree returns the tree object for origin/entire/checkpoints/v1.
   525→func GetRemoteMetadataBranchTree(repo *git.Repository) (*object.Tree, error) {
   526→	refName := plumbing.NewRemoteReferenceName("origin", paths.MetadataBranchName)
   527→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   528→	if err != nil {
   529→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get remote metadata branch reference: %w", err)
   530→	}
   531→
   532→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   533→	if err != nil {
   534→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get remote metadata branch commit: %w", err)
   535→	}
   536→
   537→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   538→	if err != nil {
   539→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get remote metadata branch tree: %w", err)
   540→	}
   541→	return tree, nil
   542→}
   543→
   544→// OpenRepository opens the git repository with linked worktree support enabled.
   545→// It uses git.PlainOpenWithOptions with EnableDotGitCommonDir set to true,
   546→// which is required for proper operation in git worktrees created via 'git worktree add'.
   547→//
   548→// Without EnableDotGitCommonDir, go-git operations in worktrees can silently fail:
   549→// - Commits appear to succeed but are not persisted
   550→// - Refs are written to incorrect locations
   551→// - The worktree's HEAD/index don't get updated properly
   552→//
   553→// This happens because worktrees use .git as a file (pointing to the main repo)
   554→// rather than a directory, and go-git needs to route paths correctly between
   555→// shared (.git/) and per-worktree (.git/worktrees/<name>/) locations.
   556→//
   557→// The function first uses 'git rev-parse --show-toplevel' to find the repository
   558→// root, which works correctly even when called from a subdirectory within the repo.
   559→func OpenRepository() (*git.Repository, error) {
   560→	// First, find the repository root using git rev-parse --show-toplevel
   561→	// This works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository
   562→	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
   563→	if err != nil {
   564→		// Fallback to current directory if git command fails
   565→		// (e.g., if git is not installed or we're not in a repo)
   566→		repoRoot = "."
   567→	}
   568→
   569→	repo, err := git.PlainOpenWithOptions(repoRoot, &git.PlainOpenOptions{
   570→		EnableDotGitCommonDir: true,
   571→	})
   572→	if err != nil {
   573→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
   574→	}
   575→	return repo, nil
   576→}
   577→
   578→// IsInsideWorktree returns true if the current directory is inside a git worktree
   579→// (as opposed to the main repository). Worktrees have .git as a file pointing
   580→// to the main repo, while the main repo has .git as a directory.
   581→// This function works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
   582→func IsInsideWorktree() bool {
   583→	// First find the repository root
   584→	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
   585→	if err != nil {
   586→		return false
   587→	}
   588→
   589→	gitPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, gitDir)
   590→	gitInfo, err := os.Stat(gitPath)
   591→	if err != nil {
   592→		return false
   593→	}
   594→	return !gitInfo.IsDir()
   595→}
   596→
   597→// GetMainRepoRoot returns the root directory of the main repository.
   598→// In the main repo, this is the worktree path (repo root).
   599→// In a worktree, this parses the .git file to find the main repo.
   600→// This function works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
   601→//
   602→// Per gitrepository-layout(5), a worktree's .git file is a "gitfile" containing
   603→// "gitdir: <path>" pointing to $GIT_DIR/worktrees/<id> in the main repository.
   604→// See: https://git-scm.com/docs/gitrepository-layout
   605→func GetMainRepoRoot() (string, error) {
   606→	// First find the worktree/repo root
   607→	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
   608→	if err != nil {
   609→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree path: %w", err)
   610→	}
   611→
   612→	if !IsInsideWorktree() {
   613→		return repoRoot, nil
   614→	}
   615→
   616→	// Worktree .git file contains: "gitdir: /path/to/main/.git/worktrees/<id>"
   617→	gitFilePath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, gitDir)
   618→	content, err := os.ReadFile(gitFilePath) //nolint:gosec // G304: gitFilePath is constructed from repo root, not user input
   619→	if err != nil {
   620→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to read .git file: %w", err)
   621→	}
   622→
   623→	gitdir := strings.TrimSpace(string(content))
   624→	gitdir = strings.TrimPrefix(gitdir, "gitdir: ")
   625→
   626→	// Extract main repo root: everything before "/.git/"
   627→	idx := strings.LastIndex(gitdir, "/.git/")
   628→	if idx < 0 {
   629→		return "", fmt.Errorf("unexpected gitdir format: %s", gitdir)
   630→	}
   631→	return gitdir[:idx], nil
   632→}
   633→
   634→// GetGitCommonDir returns the path to the shared git directory.
   635→// In a regular checkout, this is .git/
   636→// In a worktree, this is the main repo's .git/ (not .git/worktrees/<name>/)
   637→// Uses git rev-parse --git-common-dir for reliable handling of worktrees.
   638→func GetGitCommonDir() (string, error) {
   639→	ctx := context.Background()
   640→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-common-dir")
   641→	cmd.Dir = "."
   642→	output, err := cmd.Output()
   643→	if err != nil {
   644→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get git common dir: %w", err)
   645→	}
   646→
   647→	commonDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
   648→
   649→	// git rev-parse --git-common-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
   650→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
   651→	if !filepath.IsAbs(commonDir) {
   652→		commonDir = filepath.Join(".", commonDir)
   653→	}
   654→
   655→	return filepath.Clean(commonDir), nil
   656→}
   657→
   658→// GetWorktreePath returns the absolute path to the current worktree root.
   659→// This is the working directory path, not the git directory.
   660→func GetWorktreePath() (string, error) {
   661→	ctx := context.Background()
   662→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel")
   663→	output, err := cmd.Output()
   664→	if err != nil {
   665→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree path: %w", err)
   666→	}
   667→	return strings.TrimSpace(string(output)), nil
   668→}
   669→
   670→// EnsureEntireGitignore ensures all required entries are in .entire/.gitignore
   671→// Works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
   672→func EnsureEntireGitignore() error {
   673→	// Get absolute path for the gitignore file
   674→	gitignoreAbs, err := paths.AbsPath(entireGitignore)
   675→	if err != nil {
   676→		gitignoreAbs = entireGitignore // Fallback to relative
   677→	}
   678→
   679→	// Read existing content
   680→	var content string
   681→	if data, err := os.ReadFile(gitignoreAbs); err == nil { //nolint:gosec // path is from AbsPath or constant
   682→		content = string(data)
   683→	}
   684→
   685→	// All entries that should be in .entire/.gitignore
   686→	requiredEntries := []string{
   687→		"tmp/",
   688→		"settings.local.json",
   689→		"metadata/",
   690→		"logs/",
   691→	}
   692→
   693→	// Track what needs to be added
   694→	var toAdd []string
   695→	for _, entry := range requiredEntries {
   696→		if !strings.Contains(content, entry) {
   697→			toAdd = append(toAdd, entry)
   698→		}
   699→	}
   700→
   701→	// Nothing to add
   702→	if len(toAdd) == 0 {
   703→		return nil
   704→	}
   705→
   706→	// Ensure .entire directory exists
   707→	if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Dir(gitignoreAbs), 0o750); err != nil {
   708→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create .entire directory: %w", err)
   709→	}
   710→
   711→	// Append missing entries to gitignore
   712→	var sb strings.Builder
   713→	for _, entry := range toAdd {
   714→		sb.WriteString(entry + "\n")
   715→	}
   716→	content += sb.String()
   717→
   718→	if err := os.WriteFile(gitignoreAbs, []byte(content), 0o644); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // path is from AbsPath or constant
   719→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write gitignore: %w", err)
   720→	}
   721→	return nil
   722→}
   723→
   724→// checkCanRewind checks if working directory is clean enough for rewind.
   725→// Returns (canRewind, reason, error). Shared by shadow and linear-shadow strategies.
   726→func checkCanRewind() (bool, string, error) {
   727→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   728→	if err != nil {
   729→		return false, "", fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
   730→	}
   731→
   732→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   733→	if err != nil {
   734→		return false, "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree: %w", err)
   735→	}
   736→
   737→	status, err := worktree.Status()
   738→	if err != nil {
   739→		return false, "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get status: %w", err)
   740→	}
   741→
   742→	if status.IsClean() {
   743→		return true, "", nil
   744→	}
   745→
   746→	var modified, added, deleted []string
   747→	for file, st := range status {
   748→		// Skip .entire directory
   749→		if paths.IsInfrastructurePath(file) {
   750→			continue
   751→		}
   752→
   753→		// Skip untracked files
   754→		if st.Worktree == git.Untracked {
   755→			continue
   756→		}
   757→
   758→		switch {
   759→		case st.Staging == git.Added || st.Worktree == git.Added:
   760→			added = append(added, file)
   761→		case st.Staging == git.Deleted || st.Worktree == git.Deleted:
   762→			deleted = append(deleted, file)
   763→		case st.Staging == git.Modified || st.Worktree == git.Modified:
   764→			modified = append(modified, file)
   765→		}
   766→	}
   767→
   768→	if len(modified) == 0 && len(added) == 0 && len(deleted) == 0 {
   769→		return true, "", nil
   770→	}
   771→
   772→	var msg strings.Builder
   773→	msg.WriteString("You have uncommitted changes:\n")
   774→	for _, f := range modified {
   775→		msg.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("  modified:   %s\n", f))
   776→	}
   777→	for _, f := range added {
   778→		msg.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("  added:      %s\n", f))
   779→	}
   780→	for _, f := range deleted {
   781→		msg.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("  deleted:    %s\n", f))
   782→	}
   783→	msg.WriteString("\nPlease commit or stash your changes before rewinding.")
   784→
   785→	return false, msg.String(), nil
   786→}
   787→
   788→// checkCanRewindWithWarning checks working directory and returns a warning with diff stats.
   789→// Unlike checkCanRewind, this always returns canRewind=true but includes a warning message
   790→// with +/- line stats for uncommitted changes. Used by manual-commit strategy.
   791→func checkCanRewindWithWarning() (bool, string, error) {
   792→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
   793→	if err != nil {
   794→		// Can't open repo - still allow rewind but without stats
   795→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even if repo can't be opened
   796→	}
   797→
   798→	worktree, err := repo.Worktree()
   799→	if err != nil {
   800→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even if worktree can't be accessed
   801→	}
   802→
   803→	status, err := worktree.Status()
   804→	if err != nil {
   805→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even if status can't be retrieved
   806→	}
   807→
   808→	if status.IsClean() {
   809→		return true, "", nil
   810→	}
   811→
   812→	// Get HEAD commit tree for comparison - if we can't get it, just return without stats
   813→	head, err := repo.Head()
   814→	if err != nil {
   815→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even without HEAD (e.g., empty repo)
   816→	}
   817→
   818→	headCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   819→	if err != nil {
   820→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even if commit lookup fails
   821→	}
   822→
   823→	headTree, err := headCommit.Tree()
   824→	if err != nil {
   825→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even if tree lookup fails
   826→	}
   827→
   828→	type fileChange struct {
   829→		status   string // "modified", "added", "deleted"
   830→		added    int
   831→		removed  int
   832→		filename string
   833→	}
   834→
   835→	var changes []fileChange
   836→	// Use repo root, not cwd - git status returns paths relative to repo root
   837→	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
   838→	if err != nil {
   839→		return true, "", nil //nolint:nilerr // Rewind allowed even if repo root lookup fails
   840→	}
   841→
   842→	for file, st := range status {
   843→		// Skip .entire directory
   844→		if paths.IsInfrastructurePath(file) {
   845→			continue
   846→		}
   847→
   848→		// Skip untracked files
   849→		if st.Worktree == git.Untracked {
   850→			continue
   851→		}
   852→
   853→		var change fileChange
   854→		change.filename = file
   855→
   856→		switch {
   857→		case st.Staging == git.Added || st.Worktree == git.Added:
   858→			change.status = "added"
   859→			// New file - count all lines as added
   860→			absPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, file)
   861→			if content, err := os.ReadFile(absPath); err == nil { //nolint:gosec // absPath is repo root + relative path from git status
   862→				change.added = countLines(content)
   863→			}
   864→		case st.Staging == git.Deleted || st.Worktree == git.Deleted:
   865→			change.status = "deleted"
   866→			// Deleted file - count lines from HEAD as removed
   867→			if entry, err := headTree.File(file); err == nil {
   868→				if content, err := entry.Contents(); err == nil {
   869→					change.removed = countLines([]byte(content))
   870→				}
   871→			}
   872→		case st.Staging == git.Modified || st.Worktree == git.Modified:
   873→			change.status = "modified"
   874→			// Modified file - compute diff stats
   875→			var headContent, workContent []byte
   876→			if entry, err := headTree.File(file); err == nil {
   877→				if content, err := entry.Contents(); err == nil {
   878→					headContent = []byte(content)
   879→				}
   880→			}
   881→			absPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, file)
   882→			if content, err := os.ReadFile(absPath); err == nil { //nolint:gosec // absPath is repo root + relative path from git status
   883→				workContent = content
   884→			}
   885→			if headContent != nil && workContent != nil {
   886→				change.added, change.removed = computeDiffStats(headContent, workContent)
   887→			}
   888→		default:
   889→			continue
   890→		}
   891→
   892→		changes = append(changes, change)
   893→	}
   894→
   895→	if len(changes) == 0 {
   896→		return true, "", nil
   897→	}
   898→
   899→	// Sort changes by filename for consistent output
   900→	sort.Slice(changes, func(i, j int) bool {
   901→		return changes[i].filename < changes[j].filename
   902→	})
   903→
   904→	var msg strings.Builder
   905→	msg.WriteString("The following uncommitted changes will be reverted:\n")
   906→
   907→	totalAdded, totalRemoved := 0, 0
   908→	for _, c := range changes {
   909→		totalAdded += c.added
   910→		totalRemoved += c.removed
   911→
   912→		var stats string
   913→		switch {
   914→		case c.added > 0 && c.removed > 0:
   915→			stats = fmt.Sprintf("+%d/-%d", c.added, c.removed)
   916→		case c.added > 0:
   917→			stats = fmt.Sprintf("+%d", c.added)
   918→		case c.removed > 0:
   919→			stats = fmt.Sprintf("-%d", c.removed)
   920→		}
   921→
   922→		msg.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("  %-10s %s", c.status+":", c.filename))
   923→		if stats != "" {
   924→			msg.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf(" (%s)", stats))
   925→		}
   926→		msg.WriteString("\n")
   927→	}
   928→
   929→	if totalAdded > 0 || totalRemoved > 0 {
   930→		msg.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("\nTotal: +%d/-%d lines\n", totalAdded, totalRemoved))
   931→	}
   932→
   933→	return true, msg.String(), nil
   934→}
   935→
   936→// countLines counts the number of lines in content.
   937→func countLines(content []byte) int {
   938→	if len(content) == 0 {
   939→		return 0
   940→	}
   941→	count := 1
   942→	for _, b := range content {
   943→		if b == '\n' {
   944→			count++
   945→		}
   946→	}
   947→	// Don't count trailing newline as extra line
   948→	if len(content) > 0 && content[len(content)-1] == '\n' {
   949→		count--
   950→	}
   951→	return count
   952→}
   953→
   954→// computeDiffStats computes added and removed line counts between old and new content.
   955→// Uses a simple line-based diff algorithm.
   956→func computeDiffStats(oldContent, newContent []byte) (added, removed int) {
   957→	oldLines := splitLines(oldContent)
   958→	newLines := splitLines(newContent)
   959→
   960→	// Build a set of old lines with counts
   961→	oldSet := make(map[string]int)
   962→	for _, line := range oldLines {
   963→		oldSet[line]++
   964→	}
   965→
   966→	// Check which new lines are truly new
   967→	for _, line := range newLines {
   968→		if oldSet[line] > 0 {
   969→			oldSet[line]--
   970→		} else {
   971→			added++
   972→		}
   973→	}
   974→
   975→	// Remaining old lines are removed
   976→	for _, count := range oldSet {
   977→		removed += count
   978→	}
   979→
   980→	return added, removed
   981→}
   982→
   983→// splitLines splits content into lines, preserving empty lines.
   984→func splitLines(content []byte) []string {
   985→	if len(content) == 0 {
   986→		return nil
   987→	}
   988→	s := string(content)
   989→	// Remove trailing newline to avoid empty last element
   990→	s = strings.TrimSuffix(s, "\n")
   991→	return strings.Split(s, "\n")
   992→}
   993→
   994→// fileExists checks if a file exists at the given path.
   995→func fileExists(path string) bool {
   996→	_, err := os.Stat(path)
   997→	return err == nil
   998→}
   999→
  1000→// StageFilesContext describes what type of staging operation this is (for messages).
  1001→type StageFilesContext string
  1002→
  1003→const (
  1004→	// StageForSession is used when staging files for a session checkpoint.
  1005→	StageForSession StageFilesContext = "session"
  1006→	// StageForTask is used when staging files for a task checkpoint.
  1007→	StageForTask StageFilesContext = "task"
  1008→)
  1009→
  1010→// StageFiles stages modified, new, and deleted files to the git worktree.
  1011→//
  1012→// This function handles three categories of file changes:
  1013→//  1. Modified files: existing files that have been changed
  1014→//  2. New files: files that were created during the session
  1015→//  3. Deleted files: files that were removed during the session
  1016→//
  1017→// Error Handling Strategy:
  1018→//   - Individual file staging errors are logged to stderr but don't fail the operation
  1019→//   - This ensures that partial staging succeeds even if some files have issues
  1020→//   - If a modified file no longer exists, it's treated as a deletion
  1021→//
  1022→// The stageCtx parameter is used for user-facing messages to indicate whether
  1023→// this is staging for a session checkpoint or a task checkpoint.
  1024→func StageFiles(worktree *git.Worktree, modified, newFiles, deleted []string, stageCtx StageFilesContext) {
  1025→	// Get repo root for resolving file paths
  1026→	// This is critical because fileExists() uses os.Stat() which resolves relative to CWD,
  1027→	// but worktree.Add/Remove resolve relative to repo root. If CWD != repo root,
  1028→	// fileExists() could return false for existing files, causing worktree.Remove()
  1029→	// to incorrectly delete them.
  1030→	repoRoot := worktree.Filesystem.Root()
  1031→
  1032→	// Stage modified files
  1033→	for _, file := range modified {
  1034→		// Resolve path relative to repo root for existence check
  1035→		absPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, file)
  1036→		if fileExists(absPath) {
  1037→			if _, err := worktree.Add(file); err != nil {
  1038→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Failed to stage %s: %v\n", file, err)
  1039→			} else {
  1040→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Staged: %s\n", file)
  1041→			}
  1042→		} else {
  1043→			// File was deleted - stage the deletion
  1044→			if _, err := worktree.Remove(file); err != nil {
  1045→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  File not found or deleted: %s\n", file)
  1046→			}
  1047→		}
  1048→	}
  1049→
  1050→	// Stage new files
  1051→	if len(newFiles) > 0 {
  1052→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Staging %d new files created during %s:\n", len(newFiles), stageCtx)
  1053→		for _, file := range newFiles {
  1054→			if _, err := worktree.Add(file); err != nil {
  1055→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Failed to stage %s: %v\n", file, err)
  1056→			} else {
  1057→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Staged new file: %s\n", file)
  1058→			}
  1059→		}
  1060→	}
  1061→
  1062→	// Stage deleted files
  1063→	for _, file := range deleted {
  1064→		if _, err := worktree.Remove(file); err != nil {
  1065→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Failed to stage deleted file %s: %v\n", file, err)
  1066→		} else {
  1067→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "  Staged deleted file: %s\n", file)
  1068→		}
  1069→	}
  1070→}
  1071→
  1072→// getTaskCheckpointFromTree retrieves a task checkpoint from a commit tree.
  1073→// Shared implementation for shadow and linear-shadow strategies.
  1074→func getTaskCheckpointFromTree(point RewindPoint) (*TaskCheckpoint, error) {
  1075→	if !point.IsTaskCheckpoint {
  1076→		return nil, ErrNotTaskCheckpoint
  1077→	}
  1078→
  1079→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
  1080→	if err != nil {
  1081→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
  1082→	}
  1083→
  1084→	commitHash := plumbing.NewHash(point.ID)
  1085→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(commitHash)
  1086→	if err != nil {
  1087→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit: %w", err)
  1088→	}
  1089→
  1090→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
  1091→	if err != nil {
  1092→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get tree: %w", err)
  1093→	}
  1094→
  1095→	// Read checkpoint.json from the tree
  1096→	checkpointPath := point.MetadataDir + "/checkpoint.json"
  1097→	file, err := tree.File(checkpointPath)
  1098→	if err != nil {
  1099→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to find checkpoint at %s: %w", checkpointPath, err)
  1100→	}
  1101→
  1102→	content, err := file.Contents()
  1103→	if err != nil {
  1104→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read checkpoint: %w", err)
  1105→	}
  1106→
  1107→	var checkpoint TaskCheckpoint
  1108→	if err := json.Unmarshal([]byte(content), &checkpoint); err != nil {
  1109→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to parse checkpoint: %w", err)
  1110→	}
  1111→
  1112→	return &checkpoint, nil
  1113→}
  1114→
  1115→// getTaskTranscriptFromTree retrieves a task transcript from a commit tree.
  1116→// Shared implementation for shadow and linear-shadow strategies.
  1117→func getTaskTranscriptFromTree(point RewindPoint) ([]byte, error) {
  1118→	if !point.IsTaskCheckpoint {
  1119→		return nil, ErrNotTaskCheckpoint
  1120→	}
  1121→
  1122→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
  1123→	if err != nil {
  1124→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
  1125→	}
  1126→
  1127→	commitHash := plumbing.NewHash(point.ID)
  1128→	commit, err := repo.CommitObject(commitHash)
  1129→	if err != nil {
  1130→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit: %w", err)
  1131→	}
  1132→
  1133→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
  1134→	if err != nil {
  1135→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get tree: %w", err)
  1136→	}
  1137→
  1138→	// MetadataDir format: .entire/metadata/<session>/tasks/<toolUseID>
  1139→	// Session transcript is at: .entire/metadata/<session>/<TranscriptFileName>
  1140→	sessionDir := filepath.Dir(filepath.Dir(point.MetadataDir))
  1141→
  1142→	// Try current format first, then legacy
  1143→	transcriptPath := sessionDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileName
  1144→	file, err := tree.File(transcriptPath)
  1145→	if err != nil {
  1146→		transcriptPath = sessionDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileNameLegacy
  1147→		file, err = tree.File(transcriptPath)
  1148→		if err != nil {
  1149→			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to find transcript: %w", err)
  1150→		}
  1151→	}
  1152→
  1153→	content, err := file.Contents()
  1154→	if err != nil {
  1155→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read transcript: %w", err)
  1156→	}
  1157→
  1158→	return []byte(content), nil
  1159→}
  1160→
  1161→// ErrBranchNotFound is returned by DeleteBranchCLI when the branch does not exist.
  1162→var ErrBranchNotFound = errors.New("branch not found")
  1163→
  1164→// DeleteBranchCLI deletes a git branch using the git CLI.
  1165→// Uses `git branch -D` instead of go-git's RemoveReference because go-git v5
  1166→// doesn't properly persist deletions when refs are packed (.git/packed-refs)
  1167→// or in a worktree context. This is the same class of go-git v5 bug that
  1168→// affects checkout and reset --hard (see HardResetWithProtection).
  1169→//
  1170→// Returns ErrBranchNotFound if the branch does not exist, allowing callers
  1171→// to use errors.Is for idempotent deletion patterns.
  1172→func DeleteBranchCLI(branchName string) error {
  1173→	ctx := context.Background()
  1174→
  1175→	// Pre-check: verify the branch exists so callers get a structured error
  1176→	// instead of parsing git's output string (which varies across locales).
  1177→	// git show-ref exits 1 for "not found" and 128+ for fatal errors (corrupt
  1178→	// repo, permissions, not a git directory). Only map exit code 1 to
  1179→	// ErrBranchNotFound; propagate other failures as-is.
  1180→	check := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "show-ref", "--verify", "--quiet", "refs/heads/"+branchName) //nolint:gosec // branchName comes from internal shadow branch naming
  1181→	if err := check.Run(); err != nil {
  1182→		var exitErr *exec.ExitError
  1183→		if errors.As(err, &exitErr) && exitErr.ExitCode() == 1 {
  1184→			return fmt.Errorf("%w: %s", ErrBranchNotFound, branchName)
  1185→		}
  1186→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to check branch %s: %w", branchName, err)
  1187→	}
  1188→
  1189→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "branch", "-D", "--", branchName)
  1190→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1191→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to delete branch %s: %s: %w", branchName, strings.TrimSpace(string(output)), err)
  1192→	}
  1193→	return nil
  1194→}
  1195→
  1196→// branchExistsCLI checks if a branch exists using git CLI.
  1197→// Returns nil if the branch exists, or an error if it does not.
  1198→func branchExistsCLI(branchName string) error {
  1199→	ctx := context.Background()
  1200→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "show-ref", "--verify", "--quiet", "refs/heads/"+branchName) //nolint:gosec // branchName comes from internal shadow branch naming
  1201→	if err := cmd.Run(); err != nil {
  1202→		return fmt.Errorf("branch %s not found: %w", branchName, err)
  1203→	}
  1204→	return nil
  1205→}
  1206→
  1207→// HardResetWithProtection performs a git reset --hard to the specified commit.
  1208→// Uses the git CLI instead of go-git because go-git's HardReset incorrectly
  1209→// deletes untracked directories (like .entire/) even when they're in .gitignore.
  1210→// Returns the short commit ID (7 chars) on success for display purposes.
  1211→func HardResetWithProtection(commitHash plumbing.Hash) (shortID string, err error) {
  1212→	ctx := context.Background()
  1213→	hashStr := commitHash.String()
  1214→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "reset", "--hard", hashStr) //nolint:gosec // hashStr is a plumbing.Hash, not user input
  1215→	if output, err := cmd.CombinedOutput(); err != nil {
  1216→		return "", fmt.Errorf("reset failed: %s: %w", strings.TrimSpace(string(output)), err)
  1217→	}
  1218→
  1219→	// Return short commit ID for display
  1220→	shortID = hashStr
  1221→	if len(shortID) > 7 {
  1222→		shortID = shortID[:7]
  1223→	}
  1224→	return shortID, nil
  1225→}
  1226→
  1227→// collectUntrackedFiles collects all untracked files in the working directory.
  1228→// This is used to capture the initial state when starting a session,
  1229→// ensuring untracked files present at session start are preserved during rewind.
  1230→// Excludes protected directories (.git/, .entire/, and agent config directories).
  1231→// Works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
  1232→func collectUntrackedFiles() ([]string, error) {
  1233→	// Get repository root to walk from there
  1234→	repoRoot, err := GetWorktreePath()
  1235→	if err != nil {
  1236→		repoRoot = "." // Fallback to current directory
  1237→	}
  1238→
  1239→	var untrackedFiles []string
  1240→	err = filepath.Walk(repoRoot, func(path string, info os.FileInfo, walkErr error) error {
  1241→		if walkErr != nil {
  1242→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Skip filesystem errors during walk
  1243→		}
  1244→
  1245→		// Get path relative to repo root
  1246→		relPath, err := filepath.Rel(repoRoot, path)
  1247→		if err != nil {
  1248→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Skip paths we can't make relative
  1249→		}
  1250→
  1251→		// Skip directories
  1252→		if info.IsDir() {
  1253→			if isProtectedPath(relPath) {
  1254→				return filepath.SkipDir
  1255→			}
  1256→			return nil
  1257→		}
  1258→
  1259→		// Skip files in protected directories (shouldn't reach here due to SkipDir, but safety check)
  1260→		if isProtectedPath(relPath) {
  1261→			return nil
  1262→		}
  1263→
  1264→		untrackedFiles = append(untrackedFiles, relPath)
  1265→		return nil
  1266→	})
  1267→	if err != nil {
  1268→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to walk directory: %w", err)
  1269→	}
  1270→
  1271→	return untrackedFiles, nil
  1272→}
  1273→
  1274→// ExtractSessionIDFromCommit extracts the session ID from a commit's trailers.
  1275→// It checks the Entire-Session trailer first, then falls back to extracting from
  1276→// the metadata directory path in the Entire-Metadata trailer.
  1277→// Returns empty string if no session ID is found.
  1278→func ExtractSessionIDFromCommit(commit *object.Commit) string {
  1279→	// Try Entire-Session trailer first
  1280→	if sessionID, found := trailers.ParseSession(commit.Message); found {
  1281→		return sessionID
  1282→	}
  1283→
  1284→	// Try extracting from metadata directory (last path component)
  1285→	if metadataDir, found := trailers.ParseMetadata(commit.Message); found {
  1286→		return filepath.Base(metadataDir)
  1287→	}
  1288→
  1289→	return ""
  1290→}
  1291→
  1292→// NOTE: The following git tree helper functions have been moved to checkpoint/ package:
  1293→// - FlattenTree -> checkpoint.FlattenTree
  1294→// - CreateBlobFromContent -> checkpoint.CreateBlobFromContent
  1295→// - BuildTreeFromEntries -> checkpoint.BuildTreeFromEntries
  1296→// - sortTreeEntries (internal to checkpoint package)
  1297→// - treeNode, insertIntoTree, buildTreeObject (internal to checkpoint package)
  1298→//
  1299→// See push_common.go and session_test.go for usage examples.
  1300→
  1301→// createCommit creates a commit object
  1302→func createCommit(repo *git.Repository, treeHash, parentHash plumbing.Hash, message, authorName, authorEmail string) (plumbing.Hash, error) { //nolint:unparam // already present in codebase
  1303→	now := time.Now()
  1304→	sig := object.Signature{
  1305→		Name:  authorName,
  1306→		Email: authorEmail,
  1307→		When:  now,
  1308→	}
  1309→
  1310→	commit := &object.Commit{
  1311→		TreeHash:  treeHash,
  1312→		Author:    sig,
  1313→		Committer: sig,
  1314→		Message:   message,
  1315→	}
  1316→
  1317→	// Add parent if not a new branch
  1318→	if parentHash != plumbing.ZeroHash {
  1319→		commit.ParentHashes = []plumbing.Hash{parentHash}
  1320→	}
  1321→
  1322→	obj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
  1323→	if err := commit.Encode(obj); err != nil {
  1324→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to encode commit: %w", err)
  1325→	}
  1326→
  1327→	hash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)
  1328→	if err != nil {
  1329→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to store commit: %w", err)
  1330→	}
  1331→
  1332→	return hash, nil
  1333→}
  1334→
  1335→// getSessionDescriptionFromTree reads the first line of prompt.txt or context.md from a git tree.
  1336→// This is the tree-based equivalent of getSessionDescription (which reads from filesystem).
  1337→//
  1338→// If metadataDir is provided, looks for files at metadataDir/prompt.txt or metadataDir/context.md.
  1339→// If metadataDir is empty, first tries the root of the tree (for when the tree is already
  1340→// the session directory, e.g., auto-commit strategy's sharded metadata), then falls back to
  1341→// searching for .entire/metadata/*/prompt.txt or context.md (for full worktree trees).
  1342→func getSessionDescriptionFromTree(tree *object.Tree, metadataDir string) string {
  1343→	// Helper to read first line from a file in tree
  1344→	readFirstLine := func(path string) string {
  1345→		file, err := tree.File(path)
  1346→		if err != nil {
  1347→			return ""
  1348→		}
  1349→		content, err := file.Contents()
  1350→		if err != nil {
  1351→			return ""
  1352→		}
  1353→		lines := strings.SplitN(content, "\n", 2)
  1354→		if len(lines) > 0 && lines[0] != "" {
  1355→			desc := strings.TrimSpace(lines[0])
  1356→			// Remove markdown header prefix if present
  1357→			return strings.TrimPrefix(desc, "# ")
  1358→		}
  1359→		return ""
  1360→	}
  1361→
  1362→	// If metadataDir is provided, look there directly
  1363→	if metadataDir != "" {
  1364→		if desc := readFirstLine(metadataDir + "/" + paths.PromptFileName); desc != "" {
  1365→			return desc
  1366→		}
  1367→		if desc := readFirstLine(metadataDir + "/" + paths.ContextFileName); desc != "" {
  1368→			return desc
  1369→		}
  1370→		return NoDescription
  1371→	}
  1372→
  1373→	// No metadataDir provided - first try looking at the root of the tree
  1374→	// (used when the tree is already the session directory)
  1375→	if desc := readFirstLine(paths.PromptFileName); desc != "" {
  1376→		return desc
  1377→	}
  1378→	if desc := readFirstLine(paths.ContextFileName); desc != "" {
  1379→		return desc
  1380→	}
  1381→
  1382→	// Fall back to searching for .entire/metadata/*/prompt.txt or context.md
  1383→	// (used when the tree is the full worktree)
  1384→	var desc string
  1385→	//nolint:errcheck // We ignore errors here as we're just searching for a description
  1386→	_ = tree.Files().ForEach(func(f *object.File) error {
  1387→		if desc != "" {
  1388→			return nil // Already found description
  1389→		}
  1390→		name := f.Name
  1391→		if strings.Contains(name, ".entire/metadata/") {
  1392→			if strings.HasSuffix(name, "/"+paths.PromptFileName) || strings.HasSuffix(name, "/"+paths.ContextFileName) {
  1393→				content, err := f.Contents()
  1394→				if err != nil {
  1395→					return nil //nolint:nilerr // Skip files we can't read, continue searching
  1396→				}
  1397→				lines := strings.SplitN(content, "\n", 2)
  1398→				if len(lines) > 0 && lines[0] != "" {
  1399→					desc = strings.TrimSpace(lines[0])
  1400→					desc = strings.TrimPrefix(desc, "# ")
  1401→				}
  1402→			}
  1403→		}
  1404→		return nil
  1405→	})
  1406→
  1407→	if desc != "" {
  1408→		return desc
  1409→	}
  1410→	return NoDescription
  1411→}
  1412→
  1413→// GetGitAuthorFromRepo retrieves the git user.name and user.email from the repository config.
  1414→// It checks local config first, then falls back to global config.
  1415→// Returns ("Unknown", "unknown@local") if no user is configured - this allows
  1416→// operations to proceed even without git user config, which is especially useful
  1417→// for internal metadata commits on branches like entire/checkpoints/v1.
  1418→func GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo *git.Repository) (name, email string) {
  1419→	// Get repository config (includes local settings)
  1420→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
  1421→	if err == nil {
  1422→		name = cfg.User.Name
  1423→		email = cfg.User.Email
  1424→	}
  1425→
  1426→	// If not found in local config, try global config
  1427→	if name == "" || email == "" {
  1428→		globalCfg, err := config.LoadConfig(config.GlobalScope)
  1429→		if err == nil {
  1430→			if name == "" {
  1431→				name = globalCfg.User.Name
  1432→			}
  1433→			if email == "" {
  1434→				email = globalCfg.User.Email
  1435→			}
  1436→		}
  1437→	}
  1438→
  1439→	// Provide sensible defaults if git user is not configured
  1440→	if name == "" {
  1441→		name = "Unknown"
  1442→	}
  1443→	if email == "" {
  1444→		email = "unknown@local"
  1445→	}
  1446→
  1447→	return name, email
  1448→}
  1449→
  1450→// GetCurrentBranchName returns the short name of the current branch if HEAD points to a branch.
  1451→// Returns an empty string if in detached HEAD state or if there's an error reading HEAD.
  1452→// This is used to capture branch metadata for checkpoints.
  1453→func GetCurrentBranchName(repo *git.Repository) string {
  1454→	head, err := repo.Head()
  1455→	if err != nil || !head.Name().IsBranch() {
  1456→		return ""
  1457→	}
  1458→	return head.Name().Short()
  1459→}
  1460→
  1461→// getMainBranchHash returns the hash of the main branch (main or master).
  1462→// Returns ZeroHash if no main branch is found.
  1463→func GetMainBranchHash(repo *git.Repository) plumbing.Hash {
  1464→	// Try common main branch names
  1465→	for _, branchName := range []string{branchMain, branchMaster} {
  1466→		// Try local branch first
  1467→		ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName), true)
  1468→		if err == nil {
  1469→			return ref.Hash()
  1470→		}
  1471→		// Try remote tracking branch
  1472→		ref, err = repo.Reference(plumbing.NewRemoteReferenceName("origin", branchName), true)
  1473→		if err == nil {
  1474→			return ref.Hash()
  1475→		}
  1476→	}
  1477→	return plumbing.ZeroHash
  1478→}
  1479→
  1480→// GetDefaultBranchName returns the name of the default branch.
  1481→// First checks origin/HEAD, then falls back to checking if main/master exists.
  1482→// Returns empty string if unable to determine.
  1483→// NOTE: Duplicated from cli/git_operations.go - see ENT-129 for consolidation.
  1484→func GetDefaultBranchName(repo *git.Repository) string {
  1485→	// Try to get the symbolic reference for origin/HEAD
  1486→	// Use resolved=false to get the symbolic ref itself, then extract its target
  1487→	ref, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewRemoteReferenceName("origin", "HEAD"), false)
  1488→	if err == nil && ref != nil && ref.Type() == plumbing.SymbolicReference {
  1489→		target := ref.Target().String()
  1490→		if branchName, found := strings.CutPrefix(target, "refs/remotes/origin/"); found {
  1491→			return branchName
  1492→		}
  1493→	}
  1494→
  1495→	// Fallback: check if origin/main or origin/master exists
  1496→	if _, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewRemoteReferenceName("origin", branchMain), true); err == nil {
  1497→		return branchMain
  1498→	}
  1499→	if _, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewRemoteReferenceName("origin", branchMaster), true); err == nil {
  1500→		return branchMaster
  1501→	}
  1502→
  1503→	// Final fallback: check local branches
  1504→	if _, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchMain), true); err == nil {
  1505→		return branchMain
  1506→	}
  1507→	if _, err := repo.Reference(plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchMaster), true); err == nil {
  1508→		return branchMaster
  1509→	}
  1510→
  1511→	return ""
  1512→}
  1513→
  1514→// IsOnDefaultBranch checks if the repository HEAD is on the default branch.
  1515→// Returns (isOnDefault, currentBranchName).
  1516→// NOTE: Duplicated from cli/git_operations.go - see ENT-129 for consolidation.
  1517→func IsOnDefaultBranch(repo *git.Repository) (bool, string) {
  1518→	currentBranch := GetCurrentBranchName(repo)
  1519→	if currentBranch == "" {
  1520→		return false, ""
  1521→	}
  1522→
  1523→	defaultBranch := GetDefaultBranchName(repo)
  1524→	if defaultBranch == "" {
  1525→		// Can't determine default, check common names
  1526→		if currentBranch == branchMain || currentBranch == branchMaster {
  1527→			return true, currentBranch
  1528→		}
  1529→		return false, currentBranch
  1530→	}
  1531→
  1532→	return currentBranch == defaultBranch, currentBranch
  1533→}
  1534→
